So, I don't know if I'd use those exact words.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating usually happen when you're feeling stressed or upset about something?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't really noticed a specific pattern with my mood. It just sort of happens at night, regardless of whether I've had a stressful day at work or a pretty normal one. I haven't really connected it to feeling upset or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any problems with sexual function or interest?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any issues with that. Everything seems pretty normal in that department.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I haven't really paid much attention to whether my stomach is growling or anything, but... no, I don't think I'm usually actually hungry before it starts. It doesn't really feel like it's about needing food. I just kind of start eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be eating in response to boredom or some other feeling at night?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's boredom, to be honest. I mean... I usually keep pretty busy, so by the time night rolls around, I'm just winding down or watching TV. I wouldn't say I feel bored, or really any specific emotion. It's just... I just end up in the kitchen and start eating. I really can't put my finger on a specific feeling that sets it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing this eating out of habit, or is it something that you consciously decide to do each time it happens?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's a habit or a conscious choice. I just don't really know.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's like I'm watching myself do it, but I can't actually make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean, I don't feel good about it, obviously. But I haven't really stopped to think if it's guilt or shame. Mostly, I just feel frustrated with myself that I couldn't stop... and, well, I just feel pretty uncomfortable physically after eating all that. So, I don't know if I'd use those exact words.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating usually happen when you're feeling stressed or upset about something?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't really noticed a specific pattern with my mood. It just sort of happens at night, regardless of whether I've had a stressful day at work or a pretty normal one. I haven't really connected it to feeling upset or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any problems with sexual function or interest?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any issues with that. Everything seems pretty normal in that department.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I haven't really paid much attention to whether my stomach is growling or anything, but... no, I don't think I'm usually actually hungry before it starts. It doesn't really feel like it's about needing food. I just kind of start eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be eating in response to boredom or some other feeling at night?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's boredom, to be honest. I mean... I usually keep pretty busy, so by the time night rolls around, I'm just winding down or watching TV. I wouldn't say I feel bored, or really any specific emotion. It's just... I just end up in the kitchen and start eating. I really can't put my finger on a specific feeling that sets it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing this eating out of habit, or is it something that you consciously decide to do each time it happens?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's a habit or a conscious choice. I mean, I don't sit there and think, \"I'm going to go eat a lot now.\" It just sort of happens. But I also wouldn't say it's just a habit like... like brushing my teeth. It's hard to explain. I just don't really know.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an intense urge to eat when these episodes start, like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call it an intense urge, no. It's not like I feel this overwhelming pressure that forces me to do it. It's more like... I just find myself doing it. There isn't this big build-up or a feeling that I absolutely have to eat right then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to relieve some kind of tension or anxiety when you start eating like this?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't really feel anxious or wound up when it happens. I mean... my job has its moments, sure, but by the time I'm home in the evening, the day's stress is mostly behind me. I don't feel like I'm using the food to calm down or relieve any kind of tension. I just don't really notice anything like that before it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could stop eating once you start if you really wanted to?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't think I could. That's actually the main thing that brings me in here. It's... it's like once I start eating at night, I can almost step back and see myself doing it. I'll be sitting there telling myself, \"Alright, that's enough, you need to stop now,\" but my hands just keep reaching for more food. It's like I have no control over it at all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Once I start eating, it feels like I can almost see myself doing it, and I'm telling myself to stop, but my hands just keep going back for more food. I just can't seem to make myself quit once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you're eating when this happens?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel in control at all. Like I said, it's just this feeling where I know I should stop, and I'm even telling myself to stop... but my hands just keep reaching for more food. It's like I'm watching myself do it, but I can't actually make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean, I don't feel good about it, obviously. But I haven't really stopped to think if it's guilt or shame. Mostly, I just feel frustrated with myself that I couldn't stop... and, well, I just feel pretty uncomfortable physically after eating all that. So, I don't know if I'd use those exact words.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating usually happen when you're feeling stressed or upset about something?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't really noticed a specific pattern with my mood. It just sort of happens at night, regardless of whether I've had a stressful day at work or a pretty normal one. I haven't really connected it to feeling upset or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any problems with sexual function or interest?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any issues with that. Everything seems pretty normal in that department.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I haven't really paid much attention to whether my stomach is growling or anything, but... no, I don't think I'm usually actually hungry before it starts. It doesn't really feel like it's about needing food. I just kind of start eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be eating in response to boredom or some other feeling at night?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's boredom, to be honest. I mean... I usually keep pretty busy, so by the time night rolls around, I'm just winding down or watching TV. I wouldn't say I feel bored, or really any specific emotion. It's just... I just end up in the kitchen and start eating. I really can't put my finger on a specific feeling that sets it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing this eating out of habit, or is it something that you consciously decide to do each time it happens?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's a habit or a conscious choice. I mean, I don't sit there and think, \"I'm going to go eat a lot now.\" It just sort of happens. But I also wouldn't say it's just a habit like... like brushing my teeth. It's hard to explain. I just don't really know.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an intense urge to eat when these episodes start, like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call it an intense urge, no. It's not like I feel this overwhelming pressure that forces me to do it. It's more like... I just find myself doing it. There isn't this big build-up or a feeling that I absolutely have to eat right then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to relieve some kind of tension or anxiety when you start eating like this?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't really feel anxious or wound up when it happens. I mean... my job has its moments, sure, but by the time I'm home in the evening, the day's stress is mostly behind me. I don't feel like I'm using the food to calm down or relieve any kind of tension. I just don't really notice anything like that before it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could stop eating once you start if you really wanted to?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't think I could. That's actually the main thing that brings me in here. It's... it's like once I start eating at night, I can almost step back and see myself doing it. I'll be sitting there telling myself, \"Alright, that's enough, you need to stop now,\" but my hands just keep reaching for more food. It's like I have no control over it at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're following some kind of rule or ritual when you eat like this at night?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. There aren't any rules or... rituals I follow. I don't have to eat things in a certain order or use a specific plate or anything like that. It's really just... I just eat whatever is there in the kitchen. There's no real system to it at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel extremely hungry before these episodes of eating start?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I might be a little hungry since it's evening, but it's not like I'm starving or haven't eaten all day. It's just a normal amount of hunger, I guess. It doesn't feel like an extreme urge because my stomach is empty, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started, even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I mentioned a minute ago, that's exactly it. That's really the main reason I'm here. Once I start eating at night, I just can't stop, even when I want to. I can almost see myself doing it, and in my head I'm telling myself to quit, but my hands just keep going back for more food anyway. I just don't have the control to shut it down once it starts.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I just don't have the control to shut it down once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of control over eating at night is ever triggered by feeling emotionally overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. Like I said, it's just this feeling where I know I should stop, and I'm even telling myself to stop... but my hands just keep reaching for more food. It's like I'm watching myself do it, but I can't actually make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean, I don't feel good about it, obviously. But I haven't really stopped to think if it's guilt or shame. Mostly, I just feel frustrated with myself that I couldn't stop... and, well, I just feel pretty uncomfortable physically after eating all that. So, I don't know if I'd use those exact words.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating usually happen when you're feeling stressed or upset about something?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't really noticed a specific pattern with my mood. It just sort of happens at night, regardless of whether I've had a stressful day at work or a pretty normal one. I haven't really connected it to feeling upset or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any problems with sexual function or interest?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any issues with that. Everything seems pretty normal in that department.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: Not really. It doesn't really feel like it's about needing food. I usually keep pretty busy, so by the time night rolls around, I'm just winding down or watching TV. I wouldn't say I feel bored, or really any specific emotion. It's just... I just end up in the kitchen and start eating. I really can't put my finger on a specific feeling that sets it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing this eating out of habit, or is it something that you consciously decide to do each time it happens?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's a habit or a conscious choice. It doesn't really feel like it's about needing food. I usually keep pretty busy, so by the time night rolls around, I'm just winding down or watching TV. I wouldn't say I feel bored, or really any specific emotion. It's just... I just end up in the kitchen and start eating. I really can't put my finger on a specific feeling that sets it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing this eating out of habit, or is it something that you consciously decide to do each time it happens?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's a habit or a conscious choice. I just don't really know.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an intense urge to eat when these episodes start, like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call it an intense urge, no. It's not like I feel this overwhelming pressure that forces me to do it. It's more like... I just find myself doing it. There isn't this big build-up or a feeling that I absolutely have to eat right then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to relieve some kind of tension or anxiety when you start eating like this?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't really feel anxious or wound up when it happens. I mean... my job has its moments, sure, but by the time I'm home in the evening, the day's stress is mostly behind me. I don't feel like I'm using the food to calm down or relieve any kind of tension. I just don't really notice anything like that before it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could stop eating once you start if you really wanted to?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't think I could.
